"Mommy, I can't find my green crayon," Tim said. His mommy came to help him look. They looked under the bed, in his toy box, and behind the couch. But they could not find the green crayon.
Then, Tim saw his little sister, Sue. She was holding the green crayon! But it was broken in half. Tim was mad at Sue. "You broke my crayon!" he yelled. Sue started to cry.
Their mommy said, "Tim, it's okay. We can still use the crayon, even if it's broken." Tim realized that his mommy was right. He said sorry to Sue and gave her a hug. They both drew a big tree together with the broken green crayon.

Once upon a time, there was a fat cat named Tom. Tom loved to test new paths in the big park. One sunny day, he went for a walk to find a new path.
As Tom walked, he saw a big, red ball. He wanted to play with it. But when he got close, the ball started to roll away. Tom was surprised and chased the ball down the path.
At the end of the path, Tom found a new friend, a little girl named Sue. She was the one moving the ball! Sue and Tom played with the ball and had a lot of fun. From that day on, Tom and Sue were best friends, and they tested many new paths together.

Once upon a time, in a big park, there was a little ant. The ant loved to swing. Every day, the ant would go to the park and swing with an enthusiastic smile. The ant was always happy and liked to play with the other bugs.
One day, while the ant was swinging, a big wind came. The wind blew the ant off the swing and far away from the park. The ant was sad and scared. The ant wanted to go back to the park and swing, but didn't know how to get there.
The ant asked a nice bee for help. The bee said, "If you help me find my flower, I will help you find your park." The ant agreed and they worked together. They found the flower and the park, and the ant could swing again. The other bugs were happy to see the ant back. The moral of the story is that by helping others, we can also help ourselves.

Once upon a time, in a small town, there was a man named Tom. Tom had a smelly wallet. He did not like his smelly wallet, so he decided to wash it. After washing it, the wallet smelled nice and clean.
One day, Tom met a girl named Sue. They liked to play and have fun together. Tom and Sue wanted to be together forever, so they decided to marry. Everyone in the town was happy for them.
On the day of their wedding, Tom wore his best clothes and Sue wore a pretty dress. Tom remembered his clean wallet and put it in his pocket. After they married, Tom and Sue danced and laughed with their friends. They were very happy, and they lived happily ever after.

One day, a big blue ball was in the park. A small boy named Tim saw the ball and wanted to play with it. He ran to the ball and started to kick it. Tim was very happy playing with the big blue ball.
Then, a big dog came into the park. The dog saw the ball and wanted to play too. The dog ran fast and took the ball away from Tim. Tim was sad and called out, "Come back, ball!" But the dog did not listen.
Tim's mom saw what happened and had an idea. She took out a small, dead bug from her bag. She showed the bug to the dog and the dog was very interested. The dog dropped the big blue ball and went to sniff the bug. Tim got his ball back and was happy again. The dog was happy too, because it got to play with the bug.

Once upon a time, there were two friends. Their names were John and Sally. Every day, they liked to play together.
One day, John and Sally decided to play chess. They chose a special table and set up the chess pieces.
John took a small silver chess piece and threw it across the table. Sally did the same and they both laughed.
Suddenly, there was a loud noise! It was coming from outside. The two friends looked out the window and saw that it was snowing!
They went outside and felt the cold white snow. John and Sally laughed and enjoyed playing in the snow. Then, they decided to throw the chess pieces into the snow. They watched as the pieces slowly sunk in the cold snow.
John and Sally made a lot of new snow friends that day!

One day, a little girl named Ann found a troubled cat in her yard. The cat was sad and hungry. Ann wanted to help the cat, so she gave it some food. The cat was happy and said, "Thank you, Ann!"
Ann and the cat became best friends. They liked to play with a ball. They would roll the ball in a circle, and the cat would try to catch it. They played this game every day, and they always had fun.
One day, while they were playing, the ball rolled into a bush. When the cat went to get the ball, it found a big treasure chest! Ann and the cat opened the chest, and inside was a lot of gold and a note. The note said, "This treasure will end all your troubles." Ann and the cat were so happy that they had found the treasure, and they knew their troubles were over.

Once upon a time there was a little girl. She wanted to measure something, so she got some things from around the house. She grabbed a ribbon and a box.
Then she stacked the box and the ribbon in a pile so she could measure them. She used her hand to measure them. She tried to see how long the stack was.
The little girl was very happy because it was just the right size. Then, the little girl put the stack in the warm sun. She wanted the ribbon and the box to be nice and warm.
The little girl measured again and saw that the stack was still the same size, but it was much warmer. She smiled and said, "That was fun!"

Once upon a time, there was an unknown yarn. It was colorful and fun. The yarn dropped from the sky one day. It landed in a field near a big tree.
The yarn was so pretty that a little girl wanted to play with it. She ran to the tree and picked up the yarn. She started to have lots of fun playing with it, winding the yarn around the tree and making shapes for herself.
The little girl had so much fun that she dropped the yarn and ran off to play with her friends. With no one to keep it safe, the yarn was lost and unknown.
And that is the story of the unknown yarn!

Once upon a time, there was a little girl named Mia. Mia had a special cushion. The cushion could glow! At night, Mia would hug her cushion tight. The cushion would glow a brilliant color. Mia loved her glowing cushion.
One day, Mia met a boy named Tim. Tim saw the glowing cushion and said, "Wow! Your cushion is brilliant!" Mia smiled and said, "Yes, it glows at night when I hug it." Tim asked, "Can I see it glow?" Mia nodded and hugged her cushion. It glowed a beautiful color.
From that day on, Mia and Tim became best friends. They played together every day. At night, they would sit together and watch the cushion glow. They loved the brilliant colors. Mia and Tim were very happy.

Once upon a time, in a small village, there lived a selfish boy named Tim. Tim did not like to share his toys with others. One day, a big hurricane came to the village. The strong wind blew and scared everyone.
Tim ran to his friend, Sam, and said, "Sam, I am scared of the hurricane. Will you stay with me?" Sam looked at Tim and replied, "You never share your toys with me. Why should I help you now?" Tim felt sad and realized that he had been selfish.
As the hurricane passed, Tim decided to change his ways. He began to share his toys with Sam and other friends. They all played together and had fun. Tim gazed at his friends with a big smile on his face, happy to have learned the importance of sharing.

Once upon a time, in a small garden, there was a tiny ant named Andy. Andy was a dull ant, but he was very friendly. He liked to play with his friends, the bugs and the birds, in the garden.
One sunny day, Andy and his friend, Bobby the bee, found a big cookie on the ground. It was so big that they could not eat it all by themselves. Andy said, "Let's share the cookie with our friends!" Bobby agreed, and they both flew around the garden to find their friends.
They found Lucy the ladybug and Timmy the butterfly. Andy and Bobby told them about the cookie. Lucy said, "Wow! That's a big cookie! Let's all share it together." So, they all went to the cookie and took turns taking small bites. The cookie was sweet and tasty, and they all had a great time sharing it.

Lily likes to bathe in the tub. She has a lot of toys to play with. She has a duck, a boat, a ball and a bug. The bug is red and can swim. Lily calls it her water bug.
One day, Lily is bathing with her water bug. She makes it go splash and zoom in the water. She is very happy. But then, she hears a loud noise. It is her dog, Max. Max likes to chase bugs. He sees the red bug in the tub and thinks it is a snack.
Max jumps into the tub and tries to catch the bug. Lily is scared. She does not want Max to eat her water bug. She yells, "No, Max, no! That is my bug! Leave it alone!" She grabs the bug and holds it tight. Max is wet and confused. He does not understand why Lily is mad.
Mom hears the noise and comes to the bathroom. She sees Lily, Max and the bug in the tub. She is surprised and laughs. She says, "What a mess! Lily, you need to share your toys with Max. He is just curious. And Max, you need to be gentle with Lily and her bug. It is not a snack. It is a friend."
She helps Lily and Max get out of the tub and dry off. She puts the bug back in the water. She hugs Lily and Max and says, "I love you both. You are my bugs." Lily and Max smile and say, "We love you too, Mom." They are happy again.

Once upon a time, there was a little girl named Amy. She had a big box of stuff. Amy liked to play with her stuff every day. One day, she met a new boy named Tim. Tim wanted to play with Amy's stuff too.
Amy said, "You can play, but you need to know how to use my stuff. It is not easy." Tim was sad. He did not know how to play with Amy's stuff. He wanted to learn, so he asked Amy to teach him.
Amy showed Tim her stuff one by one. She said, "This is how you play with it." Tim watched and listened. Soon, he knew how to play with all of Amy's stuff. They played together and had lots of fun. Now, playing with Amy's stuff was easy for Tim. They became best friends and shared everything.

Once upon a time, there was a clever little pig. His name was Piggy and he was very brave. One day he wanted to have an adventure, so he started to search for one. He looked everywhere, but he couldn't find anything.
Then he noticed a strange case. It was very mysterious and piggy had never seen it before. He wanted to open it and see what was inside. So, he used his clever mind and started to think of a way to open it.
He thought and thought and eventually, he tried to use a rock to open it. He tried so hard and he was so determined to open the case that he started to sweat. Just when he thought he was going to give up, the case opened!
Piggy was so happy that he had solved the mystery. He opened the case and there was a magical surprise waiting for him!
The end.

One day, a helpful dog named Spot saw an automobile. The automobile was big and red. Spot wanted to act like a big dog and help the automobile.
Spot went to the automobile and said, "Hello, I am Spot. I want to help you!" The automobile said, "Hello Spot, I need help to go fast!" Spot thought for a moment and had an idea. He could push the automobile to make it go fast.
Spot pushed the automobile with all his might. The automobile started to go faster and faster. The automobile was very happy and said, "Thank you, Spot! You are a helpful dog!"
Spot felt proud that he could act like a big dog and help the automobile. He knew that even small dogs can do big things. From that day on, Spot and the automobile were the best of friends.

Sara and Tom were playing in the park. They liked to spin on the merry-go-round. Sara had a new cap that her mom gave her. It was red and had a big star on it. She was very proud of her cap.
"Look at my cap, Tom!" Sara said. "It is so pretty and shiny!"
Tom looked at Sara's cap. He felt jealous. He wanted a cap like that. He had an old cap that was brown and had a hole in it. He was ashamed of his cap.
"Your cap is not that great," Tom said. "It is too bright and silly. My cap is better. It is cool and tough."
Sara felt hurt by Tom's words. She loved her cap. She thought Tom was her friend. She did not want to play with him anymore.
"Fine, then. You can keep your old cap. I don't care. I will spin by myself," Sara said. She got on the merry-go-round and started to spin.
Tom felt angry. He wanted to make Sara feel bad. He had a mean idea. He waited until Sara was spinning fast. Then he ran to the merry-go-round and grabbed Sara's cap. He pulled it off her head and threw it in the air.
"Ha ha! Now you have no cap!" Tom said. He laughed and ran away.
Sara felt a gust of wind. She saw her cap flying away. She tried to reach for it, but it was too late. Her cap landed on a big tree. She could not get it back. She felt very sad and cried.
"Mommy! Mommy! Tom took my cap and threw it on the tree!" Sara said. She ran to her mom who was sitting on a bench.
Her mom hugged her and tried to comfort her. She was angry at Tom for being so mean. She looked at the tree and saw Sara's cap. It was too high and too far. She could not get it down.
"I'm sorry, Sara. Your cap is gone. We can't get it back. Tom was very naughty. He should not have done that. He should say sorry to you," her mom said.
Sara sniffled and nodded. She wished she had her cap back. She wished Tom was nice to her. She wished she had a happy ending. But she did not. She had a bad ending.

Once upon a time, there was a big rug in the middle of a room. A little boy named Tim and his dog, Spot, liked to play on the rug. They were calm and happy when they played together. One day, Tim saw a ball near the rug. He had a plan to have fun with Spot.
Tim said to Spot, "Let's play a game. I will throw the ball and you will hurry to get it." Spot wagged his tail, ready to play. Tim threw the ball, and it went far away from the rug. Spot hurried to get the ball, just like Tim said.
Spot was fast and found the ball quickly. He hurried back to the rug with the ball in his mouth. Tim was so happy to see Spot with the ball. They played the game again and again, and had a lot of fun. After playing, they both lay down on the rug, calm and tired. They knew they would play the game again soon.

Once upon a time there was a young girl named Sarah. She was only three years old. One day Sarah's mother put her in a bad old wardrobe. Sarah didn't like it very much. She wanted to feel free.
Suddenly Sarah heard a voice. It was a fairy! She said, "I can serve you Sarah. I can help you find freedom." And with a little sparkle, she gave Sarah a magic wish. She said, "When you open the wardrobe door, you will discover something special."
When Sarah opened the door, she found a giant garden outside. There were so many beautiful colours and smells. She began to explore and soon she was running, laughing and playing. She had never felt so free.
At the end of the day, Sarah ran back into the bad wardrobe and thanked the fairy. The fairy said, "Remember, whenever you want to feel free, come back and I'll serve you." And with that, Sarah was back in her home.

Once upon a time, there was a hairy bear. The bear liked to dance in the forest every day. One day, the mouse was watching the bear dance, and said, “Hey, Bear, why do you dance so much?”
Bear smiled and said, “I love dancing! It’s fun!” The mouse thought for a minute and said, “Do you want to try something new?” Bear said, “Sure, what do you have in mind?”
The mouse took out a long thread and said, “Let’s play this game! I will hold the end of the thread, and you will hold the other end. Then, you can dance while I make the thread follow you!”
Bear loved this idea and agreed! He danced around and the thread followed him. The bear felt so light and happy that he danced even faster. His furry tail moved around like crazy and the thread moved with him. He was having so much fun!
This became the bear and the mouse’s new game. They would play every day and had lots of fun together. It was a happy surprise for both of them!

One day, a little dog named Max went for a wander. He walked and walked until he found a big house. He smelled something very nice. It was a spicy steak. Max wanted to eat the steak, but he knew it was not his.
Max saw a big man and said, "Can I have some steak?" The big man smiled and said, "Yes, little dog, you can have some." Max wagged his tail and ate the spicy steak. It was so yummy!
But then, something unexpected happened. Max began to change. He grew bigger and bigger until he was as big as the house! Max was not a little dog anymore. The big man laughed and said, "Now you are a big dog, Max!" Max did not know what to do, but he was happy to be a big dog and play with the big man. They had fun together, and Max learned that sometimes, unexpected things can be good.

On her birthday, Lily woke up and saw her mom and dad in the kitchen. They were making her deep blue cake. Lily was so happy. She couldn't wait to eat it. But she wanted to help make the cake too. She went to her mom and dad and said, "I want to help you make my cake!"
Her mom and dad smiled and let her help. They all worked together to make the deep blue cake. Lily was very good at helping. When the cake was done, they put it on the table. Lily looked at the cake and said, "I want to eat it now!" Her mom and dad laughed and said, "Wait for your friends to come."
Soon, all of Lily's friends came to her birthday party. They played games, sang songs, and had fun. Then, it was time to eat the deep blue cake. Lily was so happy to share her cake with her friends. They all loved it. And that was the best birthday ever for Lily.

Once upon a time there were two friends. One was called Jack and the other was called Jill. Every day they liked to go for a walk down the road together. On the way there was a fair.
The fair was so exciting, it had music and games. Jack and Jill loved to go there. They would play for hours and have lots of fun.
One day, Jack had an idea. He thought it would be nice to mail a postcard to all their friends, to tell them about their wonderful trips to the fair. So, Jack and Jill decided to go to the post office to mail the postcards.
At the post office, Jack and Jill learned all about the process of mailing. They put the postcards in a colorful envelope and wrote the addresses on them. Then they put them in a big red mailbox and they rang the bell.
Jack and Jill were so happy that they had been able to mail the postcards to their friends. And they had learned a lot about the process of mailing. From that day on, whenever Jack and Jill went to the fair, they always made sure to mail a postcard home.

One day, a little boy named Tim went to play in his yard. He saw a wild tree with big, strong branches. Tim thought it would be fun to hang a swing from the tree. So, he ran to his dad and asked, "Dad, can we hang a swing on the wild tree?"
His dad smiled and said, "Sure, Tim! But first, we need to clean up the yard." Tim saw a rake near the wild tree. He picked it up and started to rake the leaves. He worked very hard, but there were so many leaves. Tim felt tired and wanted to give up.
Just then, a friendly squirrel came to help Tim. The squirrel picked up the rake with its little paws and started raking too! Tim was so surprised and happy. They worked together and soon, the yard was clean. Tim's dad came out and helped them hang the swing on the wild tree.
Tim and the squirrel took turns swinging on the new swing. They had so much fun and became best friends. From that day on, Tim and the squirrel always played together in the yard, under the wild tree.

Once upon a time, in a small house, there lived a wise old owl named Wisdom. He was very kind and liked to help others. One day, a little bird named Chirpy came to the house. Chirpy was very loud and liked to sing all day long.
Chirpy said to Wisdom, "I promise to be quiet if you help me find my way home." Wisdom thought for a moment and then agreed. They started their journey to find Chirpy's home. Along the way, they met other animals who warned them about a big, scary monster who lived in the woods.
As they walked, Wisdom and Chirpy heard a loud noise. They were scared, but they kept going. Suddenly, the big, scary monster appeared! Chirpy was so frightened that he forgot his promise and began to sing very loudly.
But then, something unexpected happened. The monster started to dance to Chirpy's loud song! The monster wasn't scary after all; he just wanted to dance and have fun. Chirpy and Wisdom laughed and danced with the monster. In the end, they all became good friends and helped Chirpy find his way home.

Lily and Tom were bored. They wanted to play, but it was raining outside. They looked around the house for something fun to do.
"Let's play with the stove!" Tom said. He saw the big stove in the kitchen. It had buttons and knobs and lights.
"No, Tom, that's bad!" Lily said. She remembered what Mommy had told them. "The stove is hot and can hurt you. And it can make fire. Fire is bad too."
"But I want to play with the stove!" Tom said. He ran to the kitchen and climbed on a chair. He reached for the stove.
"Tom, stop!" Lily said. She ran after him and tried to pull him down. But it was too late. Tom touched the stove and screamed. He fell off the chair and hit his head.
Lily was scared. She saw blood on Tom's head and hand. She saw smoke from the stove. She smelled something bad. She cried and cried.
"Help! Help!" she shouted. She hoped Mommy or Daddy would hear her. She hoped Tom was not dead. She hugged him and said, "I'm sorry, Tom. I'm sorry."

After the snack, Tim played with his toys. He pretended they were in school too. He taught them math. The toys liked math too. Tim was happy and not tired anymore.

Once there was a girl called Lisa. She wore a pretty blue skirt every day. Her mom always said it was her favourite. One day, Lisa had an idea. She thought, "What if I could make all of my friends wear blue skirts, just like mine?" Lisa got to work.
She asked each of her friends to unite, and wear blue skirts just like hers. All of her friends joined in, but one of them was a little bit foolish. She didn't want to join in. But at last, she gave in and wore a blue skirt too.
When they were all together, they looked like a rainbow. The blue skirts united them, just like Lisa had wanted. They had so much fun that day! Even the one who had been foolish laughed with the others. Suddenly Lisa knew, it was so much better to unite with her friends, than to be foolish and stay apart.

One day, a big ostrich was walking in the forest. He saw a broken tree. The ostrich wanted to help the tree. He tried to push the tree up, but he could not do it. The tree was too heavy.
A little ant saw the ostrich and said, "I can help you!" The ostrich laughed and said, "You are too small. How can you help me?" The ant said, "I have many friends. We can all help together."
The ant called all his friends. They all came and started to push the tree up. The ostrich helped too. With a loud snap, the tree was not broken anymore. The ostrich said, "Thank you, little ant. We did it together." They all smiled and went on their way.

Once upon a time, there was a cute little crab named Tim. Tim lived in a pretty shell on the soft sand. One day, Tim saw a big, bright ball in the sky. He was happy and wanted to play with it.
Tim met a new friend named Sam, who was a small fish. Tim said, "Let's play with the ball in the sky!" Sam smiled and said, "Okay, let's go!" They swam and swam, but the ball was still far away.
As they swam, the sky got dark and the ball went away. The water started to move a lot, and Tim and Sam started to tremble. They were scared. Then, a big wave came and pushed them back to the soft sand. They were safe and happy. Tim and Sam learned that the ball in the sky was the sun, and it goes away every night. They laughed and promised to play again tomorrow when the sun comes back.

Once upon a time, there was a nosy little dog named Spot. Spot loved to act in plays with his friends. They would put on shows for everyone in the town.
One day, Spot and his friends decided to act out a play about a big war. The war was between the cats and the dogs. Spot played the leader of the dogs, while his friend Fluffy played the leader of the cats. They acted out a big fight to see who would win.
In the end, Spot and Fluffy decided that fighting was not fun. They acted out a scene where they became friends and stopped the war. Everyone in the town clapped and cheered for Spot and Fluffy. From that day on, the cats and dogs in the town were friends and played together happily.

Once upon a time, there was a tall pine tree in a big forest. Many small animals lived near the tall pine tree. One day, a cute squirrel named Sally went to the tall pine tree. She wanted to send a message to her friend, a bird named Benny.
Sally climbed up the tall pine tree and called out to Benny. "Benny, I want to send you a message!" Benny flew down from the sky and sat on a branch. "What is the message, Sally?" he asked.
Sally said, "Tell our friends to come play by the tall pine tree today!" Benny nodded and flew away to send the message. Soon, all their friends came to the tall pine tree. They played and had fun all day long. And from that day on, the tall pine tree became their favorite place to play together.

Lily was a little girl who liked to play with her toys. She had a doll, a car, a ball and a gas stove. She liked to pretend that she was cooking with the gas stove, but she knew it was not real. She only turned the knob and made a sound with her mouth.
One day, Lily's mom had to go to the store and asked Lily to stay with her dad. Lily said okay and went to her room to play. She took her gas stove and her doll and put them on the floor. She said to her doll, "We are going to make a yummy soup. Do you want some?" The doll did not answer, but Lily believed that she did.
Lily turned the knob on the gas stove and made a sound with her mouth. She pretended to put some water and some vegetables in a pot and put it on the stove. She said, "The soup is cooking. It will be ready soon." She smiled and hugged her doll.
But Lily did not know that the gas stove was not a toy. It was a real gas stove that her dad had bought to fix. He had left it in Lily's room by mistake. He had not connected it to the gas pipe, but there was still some gas inside it. When Lily turned the knob, the gas came out and filled the room.
Lily did not smell the gas, because she had a cold. She did not see the gas, because it was invisible. She did not hear the gas, because it was silent. She only heard her own sound and her doll's voice in her head.
Soon, Lily's dad came to check on her. He opened the door and smelled the gas. He saw Lily and her doll on the floor, with the gas stove next to them. He was very scared. He ran to Lily and picked her up. He turned off the gas stove and opened the window. He said, "Lily, Lily, are you okay? You should not play with the gas stove. It is very dangerous. It can make a big boom and hurt you."
Lily was confused. She said, "But dad, it is not a gas stove. It is a toy. I was making soup for me and my doll. We were having fun."
Lily's dad hugged her and said, "I know, Lily, I know. But it is not a toy. It is a gas stove. And gas is not for playing. It is for cooking. But only when mom or dad are with you. Do you understand?"
Lily nodded and said, "I understand, dad. I am sorry. I will not play with the gas stove again. I will only play with my toys."
Lily's dad said, "That's good, Lily. That's very good. I love you, Lily. And I am glad you are safe."
He kissed her and took her to the living room. He gave her some juice and a cookie. He said, "Let's watch some TV, okay?"
Lily said, "Okay, dad. Can I bring my doll?"
Lily's dad said, "Of course, Lily. You can bring your doll. She is a very nice doll. And I believe she likes soup."

One day, a cat named Jazz went to play. Jazz met a dog named Max. Max was a bad dog. Jazz and Max did not like each other.
"Go away!" said Jazz. "I do not want to play with you!" Max did not listen. He kept trying to play with Jazz. Jazz was mad.
Then, something big fell from the sky. It was a big, round ball. Jazz and Max were scared. They ran and hid behind a tree. They shut their eyes tight.
The big, round ball opened up. Out came a nice bird. The bird said, "I am here to help you be friends!"
Jazz and Max looked at each other. They decided to be friends. They played with the nice bird and had a lot of fun. The big, round ball took them all for a ride in the sky.

Tom is a boy who likes to taste new things. He likes to taste sweet things, sour things, salty things and spicy things. He likes to taste fruits, vegetables, bread, cheese and meat. He likes to taste everything he can find in the kitchen.
One morning, Tom wakes up early. He is hungry and curious. He sees a jar on the table. It is red and shiny. He thinks it is jam. He likes jam. He opens the jar and takes a big spoon. He puts it in his mouth.
"Yuck!" Tom says. He spits out the spoon. The jar is not jam. It is hot sauce. It is very spicy. It burns his tongue. He feels tears in his eyes. He runs to the sink and drinks water.
"Tom, what are you doing?" his mom says. She wakes up and sees Tom in the kitchen. She sees the jar and the spoon. She understands what happened.
"I'm sorry, mom. I wanted to taste the jam. But it was not jam. It was hot sauce. It was very bad. It hurt my mouth." Tom says. He looks at his mom with wide eyes. He is scared and sad.
"It's okay, Tom. I know you like to taste new things. But you have to be careful. Some things are not for tasting. Some things are for cooking. Hot sauce is for cooking. It makes the food spicy. But it is not good for eating alone. It is too strong. Do you understand?" his mom says. She hugs Tom and kisses his head.
"Yes, mom. I understand. I will not taste hot sauce again. I will ask you before I taste something new. Can I have some jam now? I like jam. Jam is sweet and good." Tom says. He smiles at his mom.
"Of course, Tom. You can have some jam. And some toast and milk. And maybe some fruit. You need a good breakfast. Come on, let's go to the table." his mom says. She takes Tom's hand and leads him to the table. She gives him a plate and a glass. She puts some jam on his toast. She smiles at Tom.
"Thank you, mom. You are the best. I love you." Tom says. He takes a bite of his toast. He tastes the jam. He likes it. He is happy. He loves his mom.

Once upon a time, there was a fat cat named Tom. He lived in a big house with a girl named Sue. Tom loved to play upstairs in Sue's room. One day, Tom saw dark clouds outside the window. He knew it was going to rain soon.
Sue was playing with her toys downstairs. Tom wanted to tell her about the rain, so she could bring her toys inside. He ran downstairs and tried to tell her, but she did not understand him. Tom had an idea. He ran back upstairs and looked out the window.
The rain started to fall. Tom saw Sue's toys getting wet outside. He knew he had to do something. He ran back downstairs and picked up one of Sue's toys in his mouth. He carried it upstairs to show her. Sue saw the toy and understood that it was raining. She ran outside to bring her toys in before they got too wet.
Tom felt happy that he could help Sue. They played upstairs together, safe and dry from the rain. And from that day on, Sue always knew that Tom was a very helpful fat cat.

Once upon a time, there were two friends: a dog and a cow. The dog seemed lazy and was always sleeping in the sun. But the cow was very energetic, she was always playing around.
One day, the cow looked around and noticed a lot of wheat growing in the nearby field. "Look," she said to the dog. "Let's go get some of the wheat." The dog just looked at the cow lazily, but didn't move.
The cow tried to get the dog to go with her. "Come on!" she said. "Let's have some fun!" But the dog just kept looking at her. Finally, the cow gave up and ran off to the wheat field, leaving the dog behind.
The cow had a great time playing in the wheat and when she was done, she came back and showed the dog all the wheat she had collected. The dog just seemed lazy, but he was happy for his friend.

Once upon a time, in a small house, there lived a grumpy cat named Tom. Tom had a favorite toy, a ball with a zipper. He loved to play with this toy all day long.
One day, Tom's owner, a little girl named Lucy, wanted to play with the ball too. But Tom did not want to share his toy. He was very grumpy and did not allow Lucy to play with the ball.
Lucy was sad, but she had an idea. She found another ball with a zipper and started to play with it. Tom saw this and wanted to play with the new ball too. So, Lucy said, "If you let me play with your ball, I will let you play with my new ball."
Tom thought about it and decided to share his toy. They played together and had lots of fun. Tom was not grumpy anymore, and they both loved playing with their zipper balls.

As Tom marched, he met a kind dog named Max. Max saw that Tom was naughty but wanted to help him find the shiny star. Together, they marched under the night sky until they found the star. Tom learned that being naughty wasn't always good, and from then on, he marched only in the day with his new friend Max.

Spike was a big dog with sharp teeth and a loud bark. He liked to chase and bite other animals, even when they did not want to play. He did not listen to his owner, who tried to teach him to be nice and gentle.
One day, Spike saw a small cat hiding under a car. He ran towards the cat, growling and snapping. The cat was scared and tried to run away, but Spike was faster and caught her by the tail. He shook her hard and made her cry.
"Please, let me go!" the cat begged. "You are hurting me! I did nothing to you!"
But Spike did not care. He wanted to show the cat who was the boss. He was mighty and mean.
Then, he heard a loud noise. It was his owner, who had seen what he was doing. He was angry and sad. He grabbed Spike by the collar and pulled him away from the cat. He scolded him and hit him with a newspaper.
"Bad dog! Bad dog!" he shouted. "How could you do this? You are not a good friend. You are a bully. You need to learn to forgive and share. You need to say sorry to the cat."
But Spike did not say sorry. He did not feel sorry. He was still angry and proud. He did not like his owner. He did not like the cat. He did not like anyone.
He bit his owner's hand and ran away. He did not look back. He did not want to go home. He wanted to be free and wild.
He did not know that the cat was badly hurt. He did not know that his owner was worried and hurt too. He did not know that he had lost his home and his family. He did not know that he had made a big mistake. He did not know that he would be lonely and sad. He did not know that he would regret what he had done.

But when the little boy tried to get back to the green window, it was too late. He was stuck. He was so scared that he cried for help but nobody answered.

But soon, the little olive tree started to feel weak and sad. It did not understand why it felt this way. Then, an old tree told the olive tree that it needed to stand in one place to grow strong and healthy.
The little olive tree learned that it was important to stand still and be patient. It realized that good things come to those who wait. From that day on, the olive tree stood in one place, grew strong, and made many friends.

The little girl and her dad went out every day and she kept learning all sorts of amazing things. She never stopped learning, and she felt that she knew many things that she never imagined she could know.
At the end of the day, the little girl was always so happy and proud of what she had learned. She felt that she truly understood how amazing learning can be.

Once upon a time, in a small town, there was a little girl named Mia. Mia loved to play outside with her friends. One day, it was very cold, but Mia still wanted to play.
Mia put on her warm coat and went outside. She saw her friend Ben holding a triangle. "Let's play with the triangle," said Mia. Ben smiled and said, "Yes, let's play!"
They played a fun game with the triangle. They would throw it to each other and catch it. They laughed and played until they were too cold to play anymore. Then, they went inside to get warm and have a snack. Mia and Ben were happy they played together, even on a cold day.

Once upon a time, in a small village, there was a humble squirrel named Sam. Sam loved to chew peanuts all day long. He had many friends who liked peanuts too.
One day, Sam met a new friend named Tim. Tim said, "Sam, can I chew peanuts with you?" Sam replied, "Of course, Tim! Let's chew peanuts together and have fun!" So, Sam and Tim chewed peanuts and played all day.
But then, something bad happened. Sam and Tim ate all the peanuts in the village. The other animals were sad because there were no more peanuts for them. Sam and Tim felt bad for eating all the peanuts.
The other animals told Sam and Tim, "You should have shared the peanuts with us." Sam and Tim learned that sharing is important. From that day on, they promised to share everything with their friends. But sadly, there were no more peanuts to share.
The moral of the story is to always share with others, so everyone can be happy together.

Mia had a bad nightmare. She dreamed of a big, scary monster that chased her and wanted to eat her. She woke up and cried. She was very afraid.
Her mom heard her cry and came to her room. She turned on the light and hugged Mia. "What's wrong, sweetie?" she asked.
"I had a nightmare, mom. There was a monster and it was so mean and ugly," Mia sobbed.
Mom kissed Mia's forehead and said, "It's okay, honey. It was just a dream. It's not real. You are safe with me. Do you want me to rock you until you fall asleep?"
"Yes, please, mom. I like it when you rock me. It makes me feel calm and cozy," Mia said.
Mom picked up Mia and carried her to the rocking chair. She rocked her gently and sang a soft lullaby. Mia snuggled with mom and closed her eyes. She felt warm and loved.
Mom whispered, "You know, Mia, you are very unique. There is no one else like you in the whole world. You are brave and smart and kind. You can face any challenge and overcome any fear. You are my special girl and I love you very much."
Mia smiled and said, "I love you too, mom. You are the best mom ever. Thank you for rocking me and making me feel better."
Mom smiled back and said, "You're welcome, my darling. Sweet dreams."
Mia fell asleep peacefully in mom's arms. She did not have any more nightmares that night. She dreamed of happy things, like flowers and rainbows and unicorns. She learned that nightmares are not real and that mom is always there for her. She also learned that she is unique and valuable and loved.

Poppy is a dog who likes to run. She runs in the park, she runs in the yard, she runs with her friends. She is very fast and happy.
One day, Poppy sees a new dog in the park. He is big and black and has a long tail. He looks different from Poppy and her friends. Poppy is curious and wants to say hello.
She runs to the new dog and barks. "Hi, I am Poppy. Do you want to play with me?"
The new dog growls and shows his teeth. He does not want to play. He does not like Poppy. He says, "Go away, you are too small and too white. You are not like me. You are different."
Poppy is sad and scared. She does not understand why the new dog is mean. She thinks, "Different is not bad. Different is fun. I like different things."
She runs away from the new dog and finds her friends. They are kind and friendly. They like Poppy. They say, "Poppy, you are a good dog. You are not different. You are one of us."
Poppy smiles and wags her tail. She is glad to have her friends. She thinks, "I do not need the new dog. I have my friends. They make me happy."
She runs with her friends and plays in the park. She forgets about the new dog. She is very fast and happy.

Once upon a time, a cute little cat lived in a big house. The cat liked to play all day, running and jumping around. One day, while playing, she saw a new figure in the garden. It was a small, colorful bird sitting on a tree branch.
The cat wanted to be friends with the bird. She climbed the tree and said "hi" to the bird. The bird was happy to have a new friend too. They played together, chasing each other around the tree.
After a while, they got tired and thirsty. They decided to drink some water from the pond near the tree. The cat and the bird drank the cool water, and it made them feel better. They were so happy to have found each other and became best friends forever.

Once upon a time, in a small town, there lived a boy named Tim. He liked to use fancy language that no one could understand. He thought it made him sound smart.
One day, Tim met a girl named Sue. Sue said, "Hi, Tim! Want to play?" Tim replied, "Salutations, Sue! I would be delighted to engage in recreational activities." Sue did not understand what Tim said. She felt sad and walked away.
Later, Tim wanted to play with Sue, but she was scared of his fancy language. So, Tim tried to strike a ball to get Sue's attention. But the ball hit Sue's head, and she cried. Tim felt bad and said, "I'm sorry, Sue. I just wanted to play with you." Sue replied, "Next time, just use simple words, Tim."
From that day on, Tim learned to use simple words so everyone could understand him. The moral of the story is: it's important to speak in a way that others can understand, or you might hurt their feelings.

Once upon a time, there was a big gray ostrich named Ollie. Ollie lived in a nice, warm place with many trees and a big pond. He had many friends, like ducks and frogs, who played with him every day.
One day, Ollie found a big box near the pond. He wanted to see what was inside, so he tried to open it. He pushed and pulled, but the box would not open. Ollie felt sad and asked his friends to help him.
Together, the ducks, frogs, and Ollie pushed and pulled. Finally, the box opened! Inside, they found many colorful balls to play with. They were so happy and played with the balls all day long. From that day on, Ollie and his friends had even more fun together.

Once upon a time, a bald man named Tom lived in a small house. Tom was very kind and liked to help others. One day, he heard a sound coming from the forest. He went to see what was happening.
In the forest, Tom saw a little bird with a hurt wing. The bird said, "Help me, please. I cannot fly." Tom wanted to help the bird, so he picked it up and took it to his house. He took care of the bird until its wing was better.
After some days, the bird could fly again. The bird said, "Thank you, Tom, for helping me. You are very kind." Tom was happy that he could help the bird. The bird flew away, and they both had a new friend. The moral of the story is: always be kind and help others when you can.

One day, a gifted cat named Tom found a license on the ground. He did not know what it was, but he thought it was special. Tom decided to march to his friend, dog named Max, to show him the license.
"Look, Max! I found a license!" said Tom. Max looked at the license and said, "Wow! That's cool! Let's march to the park and show our friends." So, Tom and Max marched to the park with the license.
At the park, Tom and Max showed the license to their friends. The sky got dark, and it started to rain. All the animals got wet and cold. They all wished they had stayed home. The rain ruined the license, and everyone was sad.

Once upon a time, there was a pretty cat with a long tail. The cat lived in a house with a little girl. The little girl loved the cat's attractive tail. She liked to play with it and touch it. The cat did not hate the girl, but it did not like when she pulled on its tail.
One day, the cat was playing outside. It saw a big dog with a short tail. The dog wanted to play with the cat, but the cat was scared. The cat did not like the dog's short tail. It thought the dog's tail was not attractive.
The cat ran back to the house and found the little girl. The girl hugged the cat and played with its long tail. The cat did not hate the girl anymore. It was happy to have a pretty tail and a friend who loved it.

Sam and Lily were playing with their toy planes in the park. They liked to make them soar in the air and zoom around the trees. They pretended they were pilots on a mission.
"Look, Lily, my plane is faster than yours!" Sam said, as he made his plane fly high and low.
"No, it's not, Sam, my plane is smarter than yours!" Lily said, as she made her plane do loops and turns.
They were having so much fun that they did not hear their mom calling them. She was sitting on a bench with a book, but she wanted to go home. She called them louder and louder, but they did not listen. They were deaf to her voice.
"Sam! Lily! It's time to go!" she shouted, as she got up and walked towards them.
She reached them and took their planes gently. They looked at her with surprise and sadness.
"Mom, why did you take our planes?" Sam asked.
"Mom, can we play a little more?" Lily asked.
"Sorry, kids, but we have to go. It's getting late and we have to make dinner. You can play with your planes tomorrow, okay?" she said, as she smiled and hugged them.
They nodded and followed her to the car. They still loved their planes, but they also loved their mom. They learned to pay attention to her voice, even when they were having fun.

Once upon a time, there was a little boy named Tim. Tim liked to watch videos on the big TV. His favorite video was about a big, strong dog who could protect everyone in the town. The dog's name was Max.
One day, it was a mild and sunny day outside, so Tim went to play in the park. He wished that he could have a dog like Max to play with and protect him. While he was playing, he saw a small puppy all alone. The puppy looked sad and lost.
Tim decided to take the puppy home and protect it, just like Max in the video. He named the puppy Max, too. Tim and little Max became best friends, and they watched the big, strong Max video together every day.

Once upon a time, there was a hero named Tom. Tom liked to follow his cat, Spot, around the town. One day, while they were walking, they saw a big red ball. The ball was very spicy, and it made everyone who touched it say, "Ouch!"
Tom and Spot wanted to help the people in the town. They went to the ball, and Tom said to Spot, "We need to stop this spicy ball." Spot agreed, and they tried to think of a plan. They decided to roll the ball away from the town so it wouldn't hurt anyone.
But the spicy ball was too strong for Tom and Spot. When they tried to push it, they both said, "Ouch!" and had to let go. They tried again and again, but the ball was too spicy for them. They couldn't save the town from the spicy ball.
In the end, the town had to live with the spicy ball. Tom and Spot were sad they couldn't help. They walked away, feeling bad about the bad ending. But they knew they had tried their best, and they promised to keep being heroes for the town.

One day, Tom and Sue went to the museum with Mom. They wanted to discover new things. The museum had many unknown things to see. Tom and Sue were very excited.
In the museum, they saw a big dinosaur. They also saw old things from long ago. They liked the colorful paintings on the wall. Tom and Sue were having a lot of fun.
Then, something unexpected happened. They found a secret door. They opened it and went inside. There, they discovered a room full of toys! Tom and Sue played with the toys and had so much fun. They did not know the museum had a secret toy room. It was the best day ever!

One day, a colorful bird named Bob was sad. He lost his favorite toy. His friend, Cat, saw him cry. Cat wanted to comfort Bob.
Cat said, "Let's trade! I give you my ball, you give me a hug." Bob liked the idea. They traded, and Bob felt better. They played with the ball together.
But then, Dog came and took the ball. Bob and Cat were sad again. Dog saw this and gave the ball back. They all played together and had fun. The moral of the story is that sharing makes everyone happy.

Tim and Liz like to play with blocks. They build tall towers and houses and bridges. They have many colors of blocks: red, blue, yellow, green. They have fun with blocks.
One day, they find a big button in a box. The button is shiny and round. It has four holes. Tim and Liz wonder what the button does. They think it is a magic button.
"Let's press the button and see what happens!" Tim says.
"OK, but be careful. Maybe it is a bad button," Liz says.
They take the button to their block tower. They put the button on top of the tower. They press the button together.
Nothing happens. They wait and wait. They are bored.
"Maybe the button is broken. Let's press it again," Tim says.
"OK, but maybe we should move away from the tower. Maybe the button will make the tower fall," Liz says.
They press the button again. They run away from the tower.
This time, something happens. The button makes a loud noise. It sounds like a big boom. The button shoots fire and smoke. The fire and smoke hit the tower. The tower burns and breaks. The blocks melt and fly. The fire and smoke spread to the other blocks. The other blocks burn and break too.
Tim and Liz are scared. They cry and scream. They run to their mom. Their mom is angry and sad. She sees the fire and smoke. She sees the ruined blocks. She sees the button.
"Who did this? Who pressed the button?" she asks.
"Tim and Liz did it. They pressed the button. They thought it was a magic button. They were wrong. It was a bad button. A very bad button," a voice says.
The voice comes from the button. The button is not broken. The button is alive. The button is evil. The button laughs and laughs.

Tom and Sue are friends. They like to play in the park. Tom has a red vest. Sue has a blue vest. They both like their vests.
One day, they see a tall slide in the park. They both want to go on the slide. But there is only one slide. Tom says, "I want to go first. I have a red vest. Red is better than blue." Sue says, "No, I want to go first. I have a blue vest. Blue is better than red." They start to quarrel. They push and pull each other. They are not nice.
A lady sees them quarrel. She comes to them and says, "Stop, stop. Why are you quarreling? You are friends. You can share the slide. You can take turns. Red and blue are both nice colors. Your vests are both nice. But your quarrel is not nice." Tom and Sue listen to the lady. They feel sorry. They say, "We are sorry. We are friends. We can share the slide. We can take turns. Red and blue are both nice colors. Our vests are both nice. But our quarrel is not nice." They hug each other. They thank the lady. They go to the slide. They take turns. They have fun. They are happy.

Lily and Tom were playing in the garden. They liked to dig holes and look for bugs. They found a big worm and a shiny stone. They put them in a bucket.
"I need to pee," Tom said. "Can you come with me?"
"OK," Lily said. They went inside the house. They walked to the toilet. But the toilet was not there. There was a big hole in the floor.
"Where is the toilet?" Lily asked. She was scared.
"I don't know," Tom said. He was scared too. They heard a loud noise. It came from the hole.
They looked down. They saw the toilet. It was in the hole. It was very dirty. Something was lifting it up. It was a big hand. It was green and hairy. It had long nails. It was a mysterious hand.
"Help!" Lily and Tom shouted. They ran away. They found their mom. They told her what they saw.
"Mom, there is a mysterious hand in the hole. It lifted the toilet. It was scary," Lily said.
"Mom, it's true. We saw it. It was green and hairy. It was scary," Tom said.
Mom did not believe them. She thought they were making up stories. She went to see the hole. She saw the toilet. It was in the hole. It was very dirty. She saw the hand. It was green and hairy. It had long nails. It was a mysterious hand.
"Help!" Mom shouted. She ran away. She found Lily and Tom. She hugged them.
"I'm sorry. I did not believe you. You were right. There is a mysterious hand in the hole. It lifted the toilet. It was scary," Mom said.
They called Dad. He came home. He saw the hole. He saw the toilet. He saw the hand. He was brave. He got a shovel. He hit the hand. The hand let go of the toilet. It went back in the hole. It did not come back.
Dad fixed the floor. He put the toilet back. He cleaned it. He said it was OK.
Lily and Tom were happy. They thanked Dad. They hugged Mom. They did not play in the garden anymore. They played in the living room. They did not look for bugs anymore. They looked for books. They did not like the mysterious hand. They liked the shiny stone. They put it in a jar. They said it was magic.

Ben and Lily were playing in the kitchen. They liked to pretend they were chefs and make food with the pots and pans. Ben found a big glass jar on the counter. It was smooth and shiny. He wanted to see what was inside.
"Look, Lily, a glass jar!" he said. "Let's open it and guess what's in it."
Lily came over and looked at the jar. She saw a label on it that said "Honey". She knew what honey was. It was sweet and sticky and good on bread.
"No, Ben, we can't open it," she said. "It's honey. Mommy said we can't have honey without asking. It's not for playing."
Ben didn't listen to Lily. He wanted to open the jar and taste the honey. He thought it would be fun to guess how much honey was in the jar. He grabbed the lid and twisted it hard. But the lid was too tight. He couldn't open it.
"Let me try, Ben," Lily said. "Maybe I can open it."
But Ben didn't want to share the jar. He pulled it away from Lily. He twisted the lid harder. He didn't see that the jar was slipping from his hand. He didn't hear Lily say "Be careful, Ben!"
The jar fell to the floor. It broke into many pieces. The honey spilled everywhere. Ben and Lily were shocked. They heard Mommy coming.
"What happened here?" Mommy asked. She saw the broken glass and the honey. She was angry and sad. She loved her honey. She had bought it from the farmer's market. It was special honey.
"Ben and Lily, you are in big trouble," she said. "You broke the glass jar. You wasted the honey. You made a mess. You didn't listen to me. You didn't listen to each other. Why did you do that?"
Ben and Lily felt sorry. They knew they had done something bad. They didn't mean to break the jar. They didn't mean to make Mommy mad. They wanted to make her happy.
"We're sorry, Mommy," they said. "We were playing. We wanted to guess what was in the jar. We didn't know it was honey. We didn't know it was special. We're sorry."
Mommy sighed. She was still angry and sad. But she also loved Ben and Lily. She knew they were young and curious. She knew they made mistakes. She wanted to teach them.
"Ben and Lily, you need to learn some things," she said. "You need to learn to read labels. You need to learn to ask before you touch things. You need to learn to share and take turns. You need to learn to be careful and clean. Do you understand?"
Ben and Lily nodded. They understood. They wanted to learn. They wanted to make Mommy happy.
"OK, then," Mommy said. "Let's clean up this mess. And then we'll have some bread and honey. But only a little bit. And only if you say please and thank you. And only if you promise to be good. Deal?"
Ben and Lily smiled. They said please and thank you. They promised to be good. They made a deal. They helped Mommy clean up. They had some bread and honey. They hugged Mommy. They said sorry again.
Mommy hugged them back. She said it's OK. She said she loved them. She said they were good kids. She said they could play again. But only with safe things. And only with nice words. And only with happy faces. Deal?
Ben and Lily said deal. They were happy. They learned something. They guessed something. They loved something. They loved Mommy. They loved each other. They loved honey. And they loved glass. But only when it was smooth and shiny. And only when it was not broken. And only when it was not on the floor.

Once there was a little girl. She was three years old, and her name was Lily. Lily always wanted to try new things, and today was no different. She had heard about a special goal in the woods, and wanted to find it!
So Lily put on her raincoat and went into the woods. She started exploring, when suddenly she heard a loud and spooky sound. It scared her!
Suddenly she heard a voice. It said, "Why are you scared?" Lily turned around and saw an old man. He had a long beard and smelled very smelly.
The old man said, "I'm here to help you find the special goal. Do you want to come with me and find it?" Lily thought about it for a moment and then smiled. She said yes and followed the old man.
After walking for a while, they reached the goal. Inside was a big surprise. The old man said, "Now it's time to reach your goal and make your dreams come true!"
Lily was so excited and thanked the old man. She ran home and told her family all about the smelly old man and the special goal she had found.

Lily and her mum were in the kitchen. Lily saw a black potato on the table.
"Mummy, what's that?" she asked.
"It's a potato," her mum said.
"I like potatoes!" Lily said.
Lily's mum put the potato in a big pot and put it on the stove. Then, she shut the lid of the pot.
Lily watched as the pot started to shake. She heard bubbling and boiling noises from inside.
"What's happening in the pot, Mummy?" Lily asked.
"The potato is cooking," she said.
Lily smiled and waited until the potato was done. After a few minutes, her mum opened the lid and took out the potato.
It was black on the outside but soft and creamy on the inside.
Lily was so excited to eat her yummy black potato!

Once upon a time, there was a mighty dog named Rover. Rover loved to play and run in the park. One day, Rover saw a big hill that he wanted to climb. He knew that he could do it because he was a mighty dog.
As Rover climbed the hill, he found a small bag of salt. He picked it up with his mouth and carried it with him. Rover thought the salt might be useful for something later. He kept climbing the hill, feeling very strong and mighty.
When Rover reached the top of the hill, he saw his friends playing below. He wanted to be with them, so he ran down the hill very fast. Rover was so happy to be with his friends again. They all wanted to play a game, so Rover decided to part the salt into small piles for each friend. They all had fun playing with the salt and were very glad that Rover shared it with them.

Once upon a time, there was an intelligent dog named Max. Max lived in a small house with his best friend, a little girl named Lucy. One day, they wanted to go to the park, but it was raining. Lucy had an idea. She took her umbrella and said, "Max, let's go!"
At the park, they saw a big tree with a cat stuck in it. The cat was wet and scared. Max wanted to help the cat. He had an idea. He held the umbrella in his mouth and started to climb the tree. It was hard, but Max did not give up. He wanted to succeed.
Max reached the cat and opened the umbrella. The rain stopped falling on the cat. The cat was happy and climbed onto the umbrella. Then, something unexpected happened. The umbrella started to float like a magic carpet! Max, the cat, and the umbrella floated down to the ground. Lucy was so surprised! They all became friends and went home together. From that day on, Max and the cat shared the magic umbrella whenever it rained.

Lily wanted to make a card for her mom. She had paper, crayons, stickers and glue. She was very careful with the glue. She did not want to make a mess.
She drew a big heart on the paper. She colored it red. She wrote "I love you" with her crayons. She added some stickers of flowers and stars. She was very proud of her card.
She wanted to sign her name on the card. She knew how to write "Lily". She picked up a blue crayon. She was about to write on the card.
But then she saw something shiny on the table. It was a pair of scissors. She liked scissors. She liked to cut things. She forgot about the card. She grabbed the scissors.
She started to cut the paper. She cut the heart. She cut the words. She cut the stickers. She cut and cut and cut. She did not notice the card was ruined.
When she was done, she looked at the card. She saw only pieces of paper. She felt sad. She cried. She wanted her mom.
She heard her mom coming. She hid the scissors. She hid the pieces of paper. She hoped her mom would not see them.
Her mom came in. She saw Lily. She saw the mess. She saw the scissors. She was angry. She said, "Lily, what did you do?"
Lily said, "I'm sorry, mom. I wanted to make you a card. I wanted to sign it. But I saw the scissors. And I cut the card. I'm sorry, mom. I love you."
Her mom hugged her. She said, "I love you too, Lily. But you have to be careful with scissors. They are dangerous. You could hurt yourself. And you ruined your card. It was very nice. I would have loved it."
Lily said, "Can I make you another card?"
Her mom said, "Yes, you can. But no scissors this time. OK?"
Lily said, "OK, mom. Thank you."
They cleaned up the mess. They made a new card. Lily signed it. Her mom loved it. They were happy.

Once upon a time, there was a little bean. This bean was not like other beans. It was a rude bean. It liked to make fun of other beans because it thought it was the best bean ever.
One day, the rude bean met a nice bean. The nice bean said, "Hi, do you want to play with me?" The rude bean answered, "No, I am the best bean. I don't want to play with you." The nice bean was sad, but it walked away and found other beans to play with.
The rude bean saw the nice bean and others having fun. It started to feel lonely. It thought, "Maybe I should not be rude. I will try to guess what they are playing and join them." The rude bean guessed and guessed, and finally, it knew what game they were playing. It went to the nice bean and said, "Can I play with you now? I am sorry for being rude." The nice bean smiled and said, "Of course, you can play with us!" From that day on, the rude bean was not rude anymore, and all the beans played happily together.
